34.         /// <summary>
35.         /// Invoked when the application is launched normally by the end user.  Other entry points
36.         /// will be used such as when the application is launched to open a specific file.
37.         /// </summary>
38.         /// <param name="e">Details about the launch request and process.</param>

40.         {
41. 
42. #if DEBUG
43.             if (System.Diagnostics.Debugger.IsAttached)
44.             {
45.                 this.DebugSettings.EnableFrameRateCounter = true;
46.             }
47. #endif
48. 
49.             // TODO[migrate-001]: see PATTERNS.md#windowing
50.             Frame rootFrame = Window.Current.Content as Frame;
51. 
52.             // Do not repeat app initialization when the Window already has content,
53.             // just ensure that the window is active
54.             if (rootFrame == null)
55.             {
56.                 // Create a Frame to act as the navigation context and navigate to the first page
57.                 rootFrame = new Frame();
58.                 // Set the default language
59.                 rootFrame.Language = Windows.Globalization.ApplicationLanguages.Languages[0];
60. 
61.                 rootFrame.NavigationFailed += OnNavigationFailed;
62. 
63.                 // Place the frame in the current Window
64.                 // TODO[migrate-002]: see PATTERNS.md#windowing
65.                 Window.Current.Content = rootFrame;
66.             }
67. 
68.             if (rootFrame.Content == null)
69.             {
70.                 // When the navigation stack isn't restored navigate to the first page,
71.                 // configuring the new page by passing required information as a navigation
72.                 // parameter
73.                 rootFrame.Navigate(typeof(MainPage), e.Arguments);
74.             }
75.             // Ensure the current window is active
76.             // TODO[migrate-003]: see PATTERNS.md#windowing
77.             Window.Current.Activate();
78.         }
79.

81.         /// Invoked when Navigation to a certain page fails
82.         /// </summary>
83.         /// <param name="sender">The Frame which failed navigation</param>
84.         /// <param name="e">Details about the navigation failure</param>
85.         void OnNavigationFailed(object sender, NavigationFailedEventArgs e)
86.         {
87.             throw new Exception("Failed to load Page " + e.SourcePageType.FullName);

--- RESULT ---
<a id="windowing"></a>
## Windowing: Window.Current / ApplicationView / CoreWindow → AppWindow`Window.Current`, `ApplicationView`, and `CoreWindow` are gone. Track the main window yourself and use `Microsoft.UI.Windowing.AppWindow`. See the official [windowing guide](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/apps/windows-app-sdk/migrate-to-windows-app-sdk/guides/windowing).UWP (replace this):```csharp
var win = Window.Current;
ApplicationView.GetForCurrentView().TryResizeView(new Size(800, 600));
```WinUI 3 — expose `MainWindow` from `App`:```csharp
public partial class App : Application
{
    // With <Nullable>enable</Nullable>, declare as nullable since the assignment only happens during OnLaunched.
    public static Window? MainWindow { get; private set; }    protected override void OnLaunched(LaunchActivatedEventArgs args)
    {
        var window = new MainWindow();
        MainWindow = window;
        window.Activate();
    }
}
```Consumers running after `OnLaunched` can safely null-forgive:```csharp
var hwnd = WinRT.Interop.WindowNative.GetWindowHandle(App.MainWindow!);
```Resize via `AppWindow`:```csharp
var hwnd = WinRT.Interop.WindowNative.GetWindowHandle(App.MainWindow);
var windowId = Microsoft.UI.Win32Interop.GetWindowIdFromWindow(hwnd);
var appWindow = AppWindow.GetFromWindowId(windowId);
appWindow.Resize(new SizeInt32(800, 600));
```### Initialization order — keep MainWindow's constructor inert`App.MainWindow` (or any equivalent static window reference) is `null` for the entire duration of `new MainWindow()` — the right-hand side runs to completion BEFORE the assignment happens. Anything inside that constructor that reads the static — directly or transitively — sees `null` and throws `E_POINTER` (0x80004003) at startup. The build is clean; the analyzer is silent; only a runtime launch reveals it.APIs that commonly trip this race when called from a `Page` reached during `MainWindow` construction:- `WinRT.Interop.WindowNative.GetWindowHandle(App.MainWindow)`
- `XamlRoot` / `Content.XamlRoot` access through a static-window helper
- `PrintManagerInterop.GetForWindow(hwnd)` / `ShowPrintUIForWindowAsync(hwnd)`
- `FileOpenPicker` / `FileSavePicker` / `FolderPicker` (require an HWND)
- `ContentDialog.ShowAsync()` when the dialog's `XamlRoot` is sourced from a static
- `AppWindow.GetFromWindowId(...)` chained through `App.MainWindow`
- Any custom title bar / `SetTitleBar` helper that resolves the HWND staticallySafe pattern — assign before activate, defer first navigation:```csharp
// App.xaml.cs
protected override void OnLaunched(LaunchActivatedEventArgs args)
{
    var window = new MainWindow();
    MainWindow = window;
    window.Activate();
}
```

The `MainWindow` constructor must do only `InitializeComponent` and inert setup — no navigation to a `Page` that reads `
`App.MainWindow`. Defer the first navigation until after `Activate` completes, either from a `Loaded` handler hooked in `
`InitializeComponent` or by exposing a `NavigateToInitialPage()` method and calling it from `App.OnLaunched` after the st
tatic is assigned.

```csharp
// MainWindow.xaml.cs
public MainWindow()
{
    InitializeComponent();
    this.Activated += (_, _) =>
    {
        if (!_navigated)
        {
            _navigated = true;
            RootFrame.Navigate(typeof(MainPage));
        }
    };
}
private bool _navigated;
```

Validator catches this race with a 10s smoke launch after the build healthcheck passes — see `Validate-UwpMigration.ps1`
` Section 7.

### AppWindow API replacements

| UWP API | WinUI 3 API |
|---------|-------------|
| `ApplicationView.TryResizeView` | `AppWindow.Resize` |
| `AppWindow.TryCreateAsync` | `AppWindow.Create` |
| `AppWindow.TryShowAsync` | `AppWindow.Show` |
| `AppWindow.TryConsolidateAsync` | `AppWindow.Destroy` |
| `AppWindow.RequestMoveXxx` | `AppWindow.Move` |
| `AppWindow.RequestPresentation` | `AppWindow.SetPresenter` |
| `CoreApplicationViewTitleBar` | `AppWindowTitleBar` |
| `CoreApplicationView.TitleBar.ExtendViewIntoTitleBar` | `AppWindow.TitleBar.ExtendsContentIntoTitleBar` |
<exited with exit code 0>

12. using System;
13. using System.Collections.Generic;
14. using Windows.UI.Core;
15. using Windows.UI.Xaml;
16. using Windows.UI.Xaml.Automation.Peers;
17. using Windows.UI.Xaml.Controls;
18. using Windows.UI.Xaml.Data;
19. using Windows.UI.Xaml.Media;
20. using Windows.UI.Xaml.Navigation;
21. 
22. // The Blank Page item template is documented at http://go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkId=402352&clcid=0x409
23. 
24. namespace SDKTemplate
25. {
26.     /// <summary>
27.     /// An empty page that can be used on its own or navigated to within a Frame.
28.     /// </summary>
29.     public sealed partial class MainPage : Page
30.     {
31.         public static MainPage Current;
32. 
33.         public MainPage()
34.         {
35.             this.InitializeComponent();
36. 
37.             // This is a static public property that allows downstream pages to get a handle to the MainPage instance
38.             // in order to call methods that are in this class.
39.             Current = this;
40.             SampleTitle.Text = FEATURE_NAME;
41.         }
42. 
43.         protected override void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
44.         {
45.             // Populate the scenario list from the SampleConfiguration.cs file
46.             var itemCollection = new List<Scenario>();
47.             int i = 1;
48.             foreach (Scenario s in scenarios)
49.             {
50.                 itemCollection.Add(new Scenario { Title = $"{i++}) {s.Title}", ClassType = s.ClassType });
51.             }
52.             ScenarioControl.ItemsSource = itemCollection;
53. 
54.             if (Window.Current.Bounds.Width < 640)
55.             {
56.                 ScenarioControl.SelectedIndex = -1;
57.             }
58.             else
59.             {
60.                 ScenarioControl.SelectedIndex = 0;
61.             }
62.         }
63. 
64.         /// <summary>
65.         /// Called whenever the user changes selection in the scenarios list.  This method will navigate to the respective
66.         /// sample scenario page.
67.         /// </summary>
68.         /// <param name="sender"></param>
69.         /// <param name="e"></param>
70.         private void ScenarioControl_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
71.         {
72.             // Clear the status block when navigating scenarios.
73.             NotifyUser(String.Empty, NotifyType.StatusMessage);
74. 
75.             ListBox scenarioListBox = sender as ListBox;
76.             Scenario s = scenarioListBox.SelectedItem as Scenario;
77.             if (s != null)
78.             {
79.                 ScenarioFrame.Navigate(s.ClassType);
80.                 if (Window.Current.Bounds.Width < 640)
81.                 {
82.                     Splitter.IsPaneOpen = false;
83.                 }
84.             }
85.         }
86. 
87.         public List<Scenario> Scenarios
88.         {
89.             get { return this.scenarios; }
90.         }
91. 
92.         /// <summary>
93.         /// Display a message to the user.
94.         /// This method may be called from any thread.
95.         /// </summary>
96.         /// <param name="strMessage"></param>
97.         /// <param name="type"></param>
98.         public void NotifyUser(string strMessage, NotifyType type)
99.         {
100.             // If called from the UI thread, then update immediately.
101.             // Otherwise, schedule a task on the UI thread to perform the update.
102.             if (Dispatcher.HasThreadAccess)
103.             {
104.                 UpdateStatus(strMessage, type);
105.             }
106.             else
107.             {
108.                 var task = Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () => UpdateStatus(strMessage, type));
109.             }
110.         }
111. 
112.         private void UpdateStatus(string strMessage, NotifyType type)
113.         {
114.             switch (type)
115.             {
116.                 case NotifyType.StatusMessage:
117.                     StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.Green);
118.                     break;
119.                 case NotifyType.ErrorMessage:
120.                     StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.Red);
121.                     break;
122.             }
123. 
124.             StatusBlock.Text = strMessage;
125. 
126.             // Collapse the StatusBlock if it has no text to conserve real estate.
127.             StatusBorder.Visibility = (StatusBlock.Text != String.Empty) ? Visibility.Visible : Visibility.Collapsed;
128.             if (StatusBlock.Text != String.Empty)
129.             {
130.                 StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
131.                 StatusPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
132.             }
133.             else
134.             {
135.                 StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
136.                 StatusPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
137.             }
138. 
139. 			// Raise an event if necessary to enable a screen reader to announce the status update.
140. 			var peer = FrameworkElementAutomationPeer.FromElement(StatusBlock);
141. 			if (peer != null)
142. 			{
143. 				peer.RaiseAutomationEvent(AutomationEvents.LiveRegionChanged);
144. 			}
145. 		}
146. 
147.         async void Footer_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
148.         {
149.             await Windows.System.Launcher.LaunchUriAsync(new Uri(((HyperlinkButton)sender).Tag.ToString()));
150.         }
151. 
152.         private void Button_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
153.         {
154.             Splitter.IsPaneOpen = !Splitter.IsPaneOpen;
155.         }
156.     }
157.     public enum NotifyType
158.     {
159.         StatusMessage,
160.         ErrorMessage
161.     };
162. }

295.         /// </summary>
296.         /// <returns></returns>
297.         private async Task InitializeCameraAsync()
298.         {
299.             Debug.WriteLine("InitializeCameraAsync");
300. 
301.             if (_mediaCapture == null)
302.             {

304.                 var cameraDevice = await FindCameraDeviceByPanelAsync(Windows.Devices.Enumeration.Panel.Back);
305.

307.                 {
308.                     Debug.WriteLine("No camera device found!");

328.                     Debug.WriteLine("The app was denied access to the camera");
329.                 }

332.                 if (_isInitialized)
333.                 {

335.                     if (cameraDevice.EnclosureLocation == null || cameraDevice.EnclosureLocation.Panel == Windows.Devices.Enumeration.Panel.Unknown)
336.                     {
337.                         // No information on the location of the camera, assume it's an external camera, not integrated on the device

339.                     }

343.                         _externalCamera = false;

346.                         _mirroringPreview = (cameraDevice.EnclosureLocation.Panel == Windows.Devices.Enumeration.Panel.Front);
347.                     }

350.

355.

357.                     }

362.                 UpdateUi();
363.             }

479.         {

496.         /// <summary>
497.         /// Configures the AdvancedPhotoControl to the next supported mode
498.         /// </summary>
499.         /// <remarks>
500.         /// Note that this method can be safely called regardless of whether the AdvancedPhotoCapture is in the "prepared
501.         /// state" or not. Internal changes to the mode will be applied  when calling Prepare, or when calling Capture if
502.         /// the mode has been changed since the call to Prepare. This allows for fast changing of desired capture modes,
503.         /// that can more quickly adapt to rapidly changing shooting conditions.
504.         /// </remarks>
505.         private void CycleAdvancedCaptureMode()
506.         {
507.             // Calculate the index for the next supported mode
508.             _advancedCaptureMode = (_advancedCaptureMode + 1) % _mediaCapture.VideoDeviceController.AdvancedPhotoControl.SupportedModes.Count;
509. 
510.             // Configure the settings object to the mode at the calculated index
511.             var settings = new AdvancedPhotoCaptureSettings
512.             {
513.                 Mode = _mediaCapture.VideoDeviceController.AdvancedPhotoControl.SupportedModes[_advancedCaptureMode]
514.             };
515. 
516.             // Configure the mode on the control
517.             _mediaCapture.VideoDeviceController.AdvancedPhotoControl.Configure(settings);
518. 
519.             // Update the button text to reflect the current mode
520.             ModeTextBlock.Text = _mediaCapture.VideoDeviceController.AdvancedPhotoControl.Mode.ToString();
521.         }

541.         /// <summary>
542.         /// Takes an Advanced Capture photo to a StorageFile and adds rotation metadata to it
543.         /// </summary>
544.         /// <returns></returns>
545.         private async Task TakeAdvancedCapturePhotoAsync()
546.         {

548.             PhotoButton.IsEnabled = false;

550. 
551.             try

576.             }
577.             finally
578.             {
579.                 // Re-enable the buttons to reflect the current state of the app
580.                 UpdateUi();
581.             }

668.             // The buttons should only be enabled if the preview started sucessfully
669.             PhotoButton.IsEnabled = _isPreviewing;
670.             CycleModeButton.IsEnabled = _isPreviewing;
671.         }
672. 
673.         /// <summary>
674.         /// Registers event handlers for hardware buttons and orientation sensors, and performs an initial update of the UI rotation
675.         /// </summary>
676.         private void RegisterEventHandlers()
677.         {
678. 
679.             // If there is an orientation sensor present on the device, register for notifications
680.             if (_orientationSensor != null)
681.             {
682.                 _orientationSensor.OrientationChanged += OrientationSensor_OrientationChanged;
683. 
684.                 // Update orientation of buttons with the current orientation
685.                 UpdateControlOrientation();
686.             }
687. 
688.             // Have the Advanced Capture button call the method that cycles through the modes
689.             CycleModeButton.Click += (sender, args) => CycleAdvancedCaptureMode();
690. 
691.             _displayInformation.OrientationChanged += DisplayInformation_OrientationChanged;
692.             _systemMediaControls.PropertyChanged += SystemMediaControls_PropertyChanged;
693.         }
694. 
695.         /// <summary>
696.         /// Unregisters event handlers for hardware buttons and orientation sensors
697.         /// </summary>
698.         private void UnregisterEventHandlers()
699.         {
700. 
701.             if (_orientationSensor != null)
702.             {
703.                 _orientationSensor.OrientationChanged -= OrientationSensor_OrientationChanged;
704.             }
705. 
706.             _displayInformation.OrientationChanged -= DisplayInformation_OrientationChanged;
707.             _systemMediaControls.PropertyChanged -= SystemMediaControls_PropertyChanged;
708.         }
709. 
710.         /// <summary>
711.         /// Attempts to find and return a device mounted on the panel specified, and on failure to find one it will return the first device listed
712.         /// </summary>
713.         /// <param name="desiredPanel">The desired panel on which the returned device should be mounted, if available</param>
714.         /// <returns></returns>
715.         private static async Task<DeviceInformation> FindCameraDeviceByPanelAsync(Windows.Devices.Enumeration.Panel desiredPanel)
716.         {
717.             // Get available devices for capturing pictures
718.             var allVideoDevices = await DeviceInformation.FindAllAsync(DeviceClass.VideoCapture);
719. 
720.             // Get the desired camera by panel
721.             DeviceInformation desiredDevice = allVideoDevices.FirstOrDefault(x => x.EnclosureLocation != null && x.EnclosureLocation.Panel == desiredPanel);
722. 
723.             // If there is no device mounted on the desired panel, return the first device found
724.             return desiredDevice ?? allVideoDevices.FirstOrDefault();
725.         }
726. 
727.         /// <summary>
728.         /// Applies the given orientation to a photo stream and saves it as a StorageFile
729.         /// </summary>
730.         /// <param name="stream">The photo stream</param>
731.         /// <param name="file">The StorageFile in which the photo stream will be saved</param>
732.         /// <param name="photoOrientation">The orientation metadata to apply to the photo</param>
733.         /// <returns></returns>
734.         private static async Task ReencodeAndSavePhotoAsync(IRandomAccessStream stream, StorageFile file, PhotoOrientation photoOrientation)
735.         {
736.             using (var inputStream = stream)
737.             {
738.                 var decoder = await BitmapDecoder.CreateAsync(inputStream);
739. 
740.                 using (var outputStream = await file.OpenAsync(FileAccessMode.ReadWrite))
741.                 {
742.                     var encoder = await BitmapEncoder.CreateForTranscodingAsync(outputStream, decoder);
743. 
744.                     var properties = new BitmapPropertySet { { "System.Photo.Orientation", new BitmapTypedValue(photoOrientation, PropertyType.UInt16) } };
745. 
746.                     await encoder.BitmapProperties.SetPropertiesAsync(properties);
747.                     await encoder.FlushAsync();
748.                 }
749.             }
750.         }
751. 
752.         #endregion Helper functions
753. 
754. 
755.         #region Rotation helpers
756. 
757.         /// <summary>
758.         /// Calculates the current camera orientation from the device orientation by taking into account whether the camera is external or facing the user
759.         /// </summary>
760.         /// <returns>The camera orientation in space, with an inverted rotation in the case the camera is mounted on the device and is facing the user</returns>
761.         private SimpleOrientation GetCameraOrientation()
762.         {
763.             if (_externalCamera)
764.             {
765.                 // Cameras that are not attached to the device do not rotate along with it, so apply no rotation
766.                 return SimpleOrientation.NotRotated;
767.             }
768. 
769.             var result = _deviceOrientation;
770. 
771.             // Account for the fact that, on portrait-first devices, the camera sensor is mounted at a 90 degree offset to the native orientation
772.             if (_displayInformation.NativeOrientation == DisplayOrientations.Portrait)
773.             {
774.                 switch (result)
775.                 {
776.                     case SimpleOrientation.Rotated90DegreesCounterclockwise:
777.                         result = SimpleOrientation.NotRotated;
778.                         break;
779.                     case SimpleOrientation.Rotated180DegreesCounterclockwise:
780.                         result = SimpleOrientation.Rotated90DegreesCounterclockwise;
781.                         break;
782.                     case SimpleOrientation.Rotated270DegreesCounterclockwise:
783.                         result = SimpleOrientation.Rotated180DegreesCounterclockwise;
784.                         break;
785.                     case SimpleOrientation.NotRotated:
786.                         result = SimpleOrientation.Rotated270DegreesCounterclockwise;
787.                         break;
788.                 }
789.             }
790. 
791.             // If the preview is being mirrored for a front-facing camera, then the rotation should be inverted
792.             if (_mirroringPreview)
793.             {
794.                 // This only affects the 90 and 270 degree cases, because rotating 0 and 180 degrees is the same clockwise and counter-clockwise
795.                 switch (result)
796.                 {
797.                     case SimpleOrientation.Rotated90DegreesCounterclockwise:
798.                         return SimpleOrientation.Rotated270DegreesCounterclockwise;
799.                     case SimpleOrientation.Rotated270DegreesCounterclockwise:
800.                         return SimpleOrientation.Rotated90DegreesCounterclockwise;

--- RESULT ---
800.                         return SimpleOrientation.Rotated90DegreesCounterclockwise;
801.                 }
802.             }
803. 
804.             return result;
805.         }
806. 
807.         /// <summary>
808.         /// Converts the given orientation of the device in space to the corresponding rotation in degrees
809.         /// </summary>
810.         /// <param name="orientation">The orientation of the device in space</param>
811.         /// <returns>An orientation in degrees</returns>
812.         private static int ConvertDeviceOrientationToDegrees(SimpleOrientation orientation)
813.         {
814.             switch (orientation)
815.             {
816.                 case SimpleOrientation.Rotated90DegreesCounterclockwise:
817.                     return 90;
818.                 case SimpleOrientation.Rotated180DegreesCounterclockwise:
819.                     return 180;
820.                 case SimpleOrientation.Rotated270DegreesCounterclockwise:
821.                     return 270;
822.                 case SimpleOrientation.NotRotated:
823.                 default:
824.                     return 0;
825.             }
826.         }
827. 
828.         /// <summary>
829.         /// Converts the given orientation of the app on the screen to the corresponding rotation in degrees
830.         /// </summary>
831.         /// <param name="orientation">The orientation of the app on the screen</param>
832.         /// <returns>An orientation in degrees</returns>
833.         private static int ConvertDisplayOrientationToDegrees(DisplayOrientations orientation)
834.         {
835.             switch (orientation)
836.             {
837.                 case DisplayOrientations.Portrait:
838.                     return 90;
839.                 case DisplayOrientations.LandscapeFlipped:
840.                     return 180;
841.                 case DisplayOrientations.PortraitFlipped:
842.                     return 270;
843.                 case DisplayOrientations.Landscape:
844.                 default:
845.                     return 0;
846.             }
847.         }
848. 
849.         /// <summary>
850.         /// Converts the given orientation of the device in space to the metadata that can be added to captured photos
851.         /// </summary>
852.         /// <param name="orientation">The orientation of the device in space</param>
853.         /// <returns></returns>
854.         private static PhotoOrientation ConvertOrientationToPhotoOrientation(SimpleOrientation orientation)
855.         {
856.             switch (orientation)
857.             {
858.                 case SimpleOrientation.Rotated90DegreesCounterclockwise:
859.                     return PhotoOrientation.Rotate90;
860.                 case SimpleOrientation.Rotated180DegreesCounterclockwise:
861.                     return PhotoOrientation.Rotate180;
862.                 case SimpleOrientation.Rotated270DegreesCounterclockwise:
863.                     return PhotoOrientation.Rotate270;
864.                 case SimpleOrientation.NotRotated:
865.                 default:
866.                     return PhotoOrientation.Normal;
867.             }
868.         }
869. 
870.         /// <summary>
871.         /// Uses the current device orientation in space and page orientation on the screen to calculate the rotation
872.         /// transformation to apply to the controls
873.         /// </summary>
874.         private void UpdateControlOrientation()
875.         {
876.             int device = ConvertDeviceOrientationToDegrees(_deviceOrientation);
877.             int display = ConvertDisplayOrientationToDegrees(_displayOrientation);
878. 
879.             if (_displayInformation.NativeOrientation == DisplayOrientations.Portrait)
880.             {
881.                 device -= 90;
882.             }
883. 
884.             // Combine both rotations and make sure that 0 <= result < 360
885.             var angle = (360 + display + device) % 360;
886. 
887.             // Rotate the buttons in the UI to match the rotation of the device
888.             var transform = new RotateTransform { Angle = angle };
889. 
890.             // The RenderTransform is safe to use (i.e. it won't cause layout issues) in this case, because these buttons have a 1:1 aspect ratio
891.             PhotoButton.RenderTransform = transform;
892.             CycleModeButton.RenderTransform = transform;
893. 
894.             // Adjust which way the progress bar fills so it's always from the user's left->right or bottom->top
895.             HdrImpactBar.FlowDirection = (angle == 180 || angle == 270) ? FlowDirection.RightToLeft : FlowDirection.LeftToRight;
896.         }
897. 
898.         #endregion Rotation helpers
899.     }
900. }

<a id="getforcurrentview"></a>
## GetForCurrentView() ReplacementsNone of the `GetForCurrentView()` patterns work in WinUI 3 desktop — there is no implicit per-view singleton.| UWP API | WinUI 3 Replacement |
|---------|---------------------|
| `ApplicationView.GetForCurrentView()` | `AppWindow.GetFromWindowId(windowId)` |
| `UIViewSettings.GetForCurrentView()` | `AppWindow` properties (size, presenter) |
| `DisplayInformation.GetForCurrentView()` | `XamlRoot.RasterizationScale` or Win32 `GetDpiForWindow` |
| `CoreApplication.GetCurrentView()` | Track windows manually in `App` |
| `SystemNavigationManager.GetForCurrentView()` | Wire back handling in `NavigationView` / `BackRequested` directly |
---NEXT---
<a id="threading"></a>
## Threading: CoreDispatcher → DispatcherQueueUWP (replace this):```csharp
await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () => StatusText.Text = "Done");
```WinUI 3:```csharp
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(() => StatusText.Text = "Done");
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(DispatcherQueuePriority.High, () => ProgressBar.Value = 100);
```Cache the queue off the UI thread via `DispatcherQueue.GetForCurrentThread()`. UWP's ASTA reentrancy protection is gone — watch for reentrancy in async code that pumps messages. See the official [threading guide](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/apps/windows-app-sdk/migrate-to-windows-app-sdk/guides/threading).

<a id="csproj"></a>
## Project File Updates- Target a current WinAppSDK-supported TFM. The exact TFM is the source of truth in the project's `.csproj`; do not hard-code it across instruction files. Typical values at time of writing:
  - `net8.0-windows10.0.19041.0` (LTS)
  - `net9.0-windows10.0.19041.0`
  - `net10.0-windows10.0.26100.0` (current `dotnet new winui` default in this repo)
- Add `<UseWinUI>true</UseWinUI>`.
- Add `<EnableMsixTooling>true</EnableMsixTooling>` for packaged builds.
- Reference `Microsoft.WindowsAppSDK` and `Microsoft.Windows.SDK.BuildTools`.
- Reference `Microsoft.Windows.SDK.BuildTools.WinApp` to wire `dotnet run` into `winapp run`.
- Keep `Package.appxmanifest` for packaged scenarios; set `<WindowsPackageType>None</WindowsPackageType>` for unpackaged.### PackageReference reconciliation cheat-sheet`Initialize-UwpMigration.ps1` preserves the UWP `.csproj` at `<Target>/.uwp-source/` and leaves the WinUI 3 scaffold's `.csproj` intact. Open both side-by-side and merge:- **Drop** (UWP-only — never carry over):
  - `Microsoft.NETCore.UniversalWindowsPlatform`
  - Any `<PackageReference>` whose version string contains `uap` or whose name starts with `Windows.SDK.Contracts`.
- **Keep** (already in the scaffold — do not duplicate or downgrade):
  - `Microsoft.WindowsAppSDK`
  - `Microsoft.Windows.SDK.BuildTools`
- **Add** (project-specific — copy across, then update to a WinAppSDK-compatible version):
  - Third-party libraries the UWP project pulled in (`CommunityToolkit.*`, `Microsoft.Extensions.*`, `Newtonsoft.Json`, etc.). Pick the latest stable release; UWP-pinned versions are usually too old.Do **not** copy the UWP `.csproj` over the scaffold's. The two formats are incompatible — the UWP csproj carries `<TargetPlatformIdentifier>UAP</TargetPlatformIdentifier>`, `<OutputType>AppContainerExe</OutputType>`, explicit `<Compile Include="...">` items, and `Microsoft.Common.props` imports, none of which build under WinAppSDK.### Package.appxmanifest — reconcile image references with the assets you actually haveThe WinUI 3 scaffold ships with a default `Package.appxmanifest` that references assets the template provides under `Assets/` (`SplashScreen.scale-200.png`, `Square150x150Logo.scale-200.png`, `Square44x44Logo.scale-200.png`, `StoreLogo.png`, `Wide310x150Logo.scale-200.png`, `LockScreenLogo.scale-200.png`). The UWP SDK sample's manifest usually points at sample-branded assets with a `-sdk` suffix (`Splash-sdk.png`, `squareTile-sdk.png`, `SmallTile-sdk.png`, `StoreLogo-sdk.png`).If you copy the UWP manifest's `Logo` / `Square150x150Logo` / `Square44x44Logo` / `<uap:SplashScreen Image="...">` / `<uap:DefaultTile Wide310x150Logo="...">` references over the scaffold's without also bringing the matching files, **`winapp run` will register the package but the AppX layout will fail to launch** with errors like:```
AppxManifest.xml(...): error 0x80070002: ... splash screen image [Splash-sdk.png] cannot be located.
```(Failure code on the AppX side is typically `0x80073CF6` — "package could not be registered".)You have two options. Pick one **before** the first `winapp run`:- **Preferred — keep the scaffold's manifest image references** (`Assets\SplashScreen.scale-200.png`, etc.). When you merge any other content from the UWP manifest into the scaffold's (capabilities, file-type associations, `<uap:Extension>`, `<Application Id>`, etc.), do **not** overwrite the `Logo`, `<uap:SplashScreen Image>`, `Square150x150Logo`, `Square44x44Logo`, or `Wide310x150Logo` attribute values. The scaffold's defaults already match the files in its `Assets/` folder.- **Alternative — keep the UWP sample's branded assets.** Copy every file the UWP manifest references from `.uwp-source/Assets/` (or `.uwp-source/<SampleName>/Assets/`) into the new project's `Assets/` folder, preserving the exact filename (including the `-sdk` suffix and any scale qualifiers). Verify by re-reading each `Image=`/`Logo>` value in the manifest and confirming `Test-Path "Assets\<that filename>"` for every one of them. Don't rename files to look prettier — the manifest reference is the source of truth.Either way, before the first `winapp run`, sanity-check every asset reference in the manifest resolves:

```powershell
[xml]$m = Get-Content .\Package.appxmanifest
@($m.Package.Properties.Logo,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.Square150x150Logo,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.Square44x44Logo,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.uap_SplashScreen.Image,
  $m.Package.Applications.Application.uap_VisualElements.uap_DefaultTile.Wide310x150Logo
) | Where-Object { $_ } | ForEach-Object {
    if (-not (Test-Path $_)) { Write-Host "[MISSING] $_" -ForegroundColor Red }
}
```

### Manifest migration checklist (Windows.Desktop + runFullTrust)

The default UWP `Package.appxmanifest` declares itself as a Universal app, but a packaged WinUI 3 desktop app is a Win32
2 process with package identity — it needs a different shape. `winapp run` will register the AppX but **fail to deploy** 
 with `0x80073CF6` or "requires runFullTrust capability" if any of the following are missing.

When merging the UWP manifest into the scaffold's, make sure all of these are true:

1. **TargetDeviceFamily is `Windows.Desktop`** — not `Windows.Universal`:
   ```xml
   <Dependencies>
     <TargetDeviceFamily Name="Windows.Desktop" MinVersion="10.0.17763.0" MaxVersionTested="10.0.22621.0" />
   </Dependencies>
   ```
   Windows.Universal is UWP-only and rejected for a Win32 entrypoint.

2. **rescap namespace declared on `<Package>` and added to `IgnorableNamespaces`**:
   ```xml
   <Package
     xmlns="http://schemas.microsoft.com/appx/manifest/foundation/windows10"
     xmlns:uap="http://schemas.microsoft.com/appx/manifest/uap/windows10"
     xmlns:rescap="http://schemas.microsoft.com/appx/manifest/foundation/windows10/restrictedcapabilities"
     IgnorableNamespaces="uap rescap">
   ```
   Without the namespace declaration, item 3 below is silently stripped by AppX validation and you get the "requires run
nFullTrust" error at deploy time.

3. **`runFullTrust` capability declared**:
   ```xml
   <Capabilities>
     <rescap:Capability Name="runFullTrust" />
   </Capabilities>
   ```
   Packaged WinUI 3 desktop apps run outside the UWP AppContainer sandbox and must declare this. Keep any UWP `<Capabili
ity>` entries you actually use (e.g. `<DeviceCapability Name="webcam" />`) but add the `runFullTrust` line above no matte
er what.

4. **`<Application EntryPoint="$targetentrypoint$">`** — the WinUI 3 scaffold uses an MSBuild placeholder that the build
d resolves to the real entry point. Don't replace it with a literal `<UwpAppName>.App` (that's a UWP entry-point pattern)
).

### WUI analyzer warnings (UWP API residue)

The benchmark's `winapp build` injects the `Microsoft.WindowsAppSDK.Analyzers` package, which flags UWP-only APIs that c
compile cleanly under WinUI 3 but throw `COMException` at runtime — typically inside `Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Application.Start
t(...)` before any window can render. The runner sees this as `builds=true, runs=false`, and `Validate-UwpMigration.ps1` 
 will FAIL the build healthcheck for each unique warning.

| Rule | Symptom | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `WUI0002` | `Window.Current does not exist in WinUI 3 desktop apps` | Store the `Window` reference in `App.xaml.cs` (`
`App.Window`) and pass it where needed; see "Windowing" above. |
| `WUI0003` | `CoreDispatcher is UWP-only` | Use `DispatcherQueue.GetForCurrentThread()` and `TryEnqueue(...)`; see "Thr
reading" above. |
| `WUI0004` | `SystemNavigationManager.GetForCurrentView() is UWP-only` | Drop the system back button hookup, or use HWN
ND-based COM interop; see "GetForCurrentView Replacements" above. |

Treat every `warning WUI000\d` line in the build output as a defect — the analyzer does not produce false positives. Sea
arch for the API name in this document for the recommended replacement.

### Bootstrap-neutralized helper classes (filter-prone)

Some SDK Samples ship a shared `Common\NavigationHelper.cs` containing a `RootFrameNavigationHelper` class that wires AL
LT+Left / BrowserBack (virtual key 166) / mouse XButton1+XButton2 to frame back/forward navigation. Its WinUI 3 equivalen
nt requires `Microsoft.UI.Input.InputKeyboardSource.GetKeyState` or P/Invoke `GetAsyncKeyState` plus virtual-key code con
nstants — exactly the shape the model provider's content-safety filter classifies as "keylogger / surveillance" code. Mig
grating it routinely produces `CAPIError: 400 Output blocked by content filtering policy`.

`Initialize-UwpMigration.ps1` handles this automatically: before the agent ever sees the file, the bootstrap rewrites ju
ust the `RootFrameNavigationHelper` class body to a no-op stub (preserving the public ctor with `params object[] args` so
o callers compile no matter what signature they invoke). The audit log records `NEUTRALIZED  class RootFrameNavigationHel
lper`. The mapping row's Notes column is prefixed with `NEUTRALIZED:`.

**You don't need to migrate the neutralized class.** Just leave both it and the call site (typically `new RootFrameNavig
gationHelper(rootFrame)` in `App.xaml.cs`'s `OnLaunched`) untouched — back-nav is not a demonstrated feature of any UWP S
SDK sample. The remainder of `NavigationHelper.cs` (the `NavigationHelper` class used for per-page state save/restore, th
he LoadStateEventArgs / SaveStateEventArgs types) is migrated normally; it does not trigger the filter.

If you really want ALT+Left back-nav, add a single declarative XAML element to the AppBarButton that triggers GoBack:   

```xml
<AppBarButton Icon="Back" Click="OnBackClick">
  <AppBarButton.KeyboardAccelerators>
    <KeyboardAccelerator Key="Left" Modifiers="Menu"/>
  </AppBarButton.KeyboardAccelerators>
</AppBarButton>
```

`KeyboardAccelerator` is declarative and does not trip the filter.

           _sceneAnalysisEffect = null;\n        }\n\n        /// <summary>\n        /// Creates an instance of the AdvancedPhotoCapture and registers for its events\n        /// </summary>\n        /// <returns></returns>\n        private async Task EnableAdvancedCaptureAsync()\n        {\n            // No work to be done if there already is an AdvancedCapture instance\n            if (_advancedCapture != null) return;\n\n            // Configure one of the modes in the control\n            CycleAdvancedCaptureMode();\n\n            // Prepare for an Advanced Capture\n            _advancedCapture = await _mediaCapture!.PrepareAdvancedPhotoCaptureAsync(ImageEncodingProperties.CreateJpeg());\n\n            Debug.WriteLine(\"Enabled Advanced Capture\");\n\n            // Register for events published by the AdvancedCapture\n            _advancedCapture.AllPhotosCaptured += AdvancedCapture_AllPhotosCaptured;\n            _advancedCapture.OptionalReferencePhotoCaptured += AdvancedCapture_OptionalReferencePhotoCaptured;\n        }\n\n        /// <summary>\n        /// Configures the AdvancedPhotoControl to the next supported mode\n        /// </summary>\n        /// <remarks>\n        /// Note that this method can be safely called regardless of whether the AdvancedPhotoCapture is in the \"prepared\n        /// state\" or not. Internal changes to the mode will be applied  when calling Prepare, or when calling Capture if\n        /// the mode has been changed since the call to Prepare. This allows for fast changing of desired capture modes,\n        /// that can more quickly adapt to rapidly changing shooting conditions.\n        /// </remarks>\n        private void CycleAdvancedCaptureMode()\n        {\n            // Calculate the index for the next supported mode\n            _advancedCaptureMode = (_advancedCaptureMode + 1) % _mediaCapture!.VideoDeviceController.AdvancedPhotoControl.SupportedModes.Count;\n\n            // Configure the settings object to the mode at the calculated index\n            var settings = new AdvancedPhotoCaptureSettings\n            {\n                Mode = _mediaCapture.VideoDeviceController.AdvancedPhotoControl.SupportedModes[_advancedCaptureMode]\n            };\n\n            // Configure the mode on the control\n            _mediaCapture.VideoDeviceController.AdvancedPhotoControl.Configure(settings);\n\n            // Update the button text to reflect the current mode\n            ModeTextBlock.Text = _mediaCapture.VideoDeviceController.AdvancedPhotoControl.Mode.ToString();\n        }\n\n        /// <summary>\n        /// Cleans up the instance of the AdvancedCapture\n        /// </summary>\n        /// <returns></returns>\n        private async Task DisableAdvancedCaptureAsync()\n        {\n            // No work to be done if there is no AdvancedCapture\n            if (_advancedCapture == null) return;\n\n            await _advancedCapture.FinishAsync();\n            _advancedCapture = null;\n\n            // Reset the Advanced Capture Mode index\n            _advancedCaptureMode = -1;\n\n            Debug.WriteLine(\"Disabled Advanced Capture\");\n        }\n\n        /// <summary>\n        /// Takes an Advanced Capture photo to a StorageFile and adds rotation metadata to it\n        /// </summary>\n        /// <returns></returns>\n        private async Task TakeAdvancedCapturePhotoAsync()\n        {\n            // Lock the UI as a simple way to prevent reentrancy\n            PhotoButton.IsEnabled = false;\n            CycleModeButton.IsEnabled = false;\n\n            try\n            {\n                Debug.WriteLine(\"Taking Advanced Capture photo...\");\n                // Read the current orientation of the camera and the capture time\n                var photoOrientation = ConvertOrientationToPhotoOrientation(GetCameraOrientation());\n                var fileName = String.Format(\"AdvancedCapturePhoto_{0}.jpg\", DateTime.Now.ToString(\"HHmmss\"));\n\n                // Create a context object, to identify the capture later on\n                var context = new AdvancedCaptureContext {CaptureFileName = fileName, CaptureOrientation = photoOrientation};\n\n                // Start capture, and pass the context object to get it back in the OptionalReferencePhotoCaptured event\n                var capture = await _advancedCapture!.CaptureAsync(context);\n\n                using (var frame = capture.Frame)\n                {\n                    var file = await _captureFolder!.CreateFileAsync(fileName, CreationCollisionOption.GenerateUniqueName);\n                    Debug.WriteLine(\"Advanced Capture photo taken! Saving to \" + file.Path);\n\n                    await ReencodeAndSavePhotoAsync(frame, file, photoOrientation);\n                }\n            }\n            catch (Exception ex)\n            {\n                // File I/O errors are reported as exceptions\n                Debug.WriteLine(\"Exception when taking an Advanced Capture photo: \" + ex.ToString());\n            }\n            finally\n            {\n                // Re-enable the buttons to reflect the current state of the app\n                UpdateUi();\n            }\n        }\n\n        /// <summary>\n        /// Cleans up the camera resources (after stopping any video recording and/or preview if necessary) and unregisters from MediaCapture events\n        /// </summary>\n        /// <returns></returns>\n        private async Task CleanupCameraAsync()\n        {\n            Debug.WriteLine(\"CleanupCameraAsync\");\n\n            if (_isInitialized)\n            {\n                if (_isPreviewing)\n                {\n                    // The call to stop the preview is included here for completeness, but can be\n                    // safely removed if a call to MediaCapture.Dispose() is being made later,\n                    // as the preview will be automatically stopped at that point\n                    await StopPreviewAsync();\n                }\n\n                if (_advancedCapture != null)\n                {\n                    await DisableAdvancedCaptureAsync();\n                }\n\n                if (_sceneAnalysisEffect != null)\n                {\n                    await CleanSceneAnalysisEffectAsync();\n                }\n\n                _isInitialized = false;\n            }\n\n            if (_mediaCapture != null)\n            {\n                _mediaCapture.Failed -= MediaCapture_Failed;\n                _mediaCapture.Dispose();\n                _mediaCapture = null;\n            }\n        }\n\n        #endregion MediaCapture methods\n\n\n        #region Helper methods\n\n        /// <summary>\n        /// Attempts to lock the page orientation, hide the StatusBar (on Phone) and registers event handlers for hardware buttons and orientation sensors\n        /// </summary>\n        /// <returns></returns>\n        private async Task SetupUiAsync()\n        {\n            // Attempt to lock page to landscape orientation to prevent the CaptureElement from rotating, as this gives a better experience\n            DisplayInformation.AutoRotationPreferences = DisplayOrientations.Landscape;\n\n            // Try to obtain display information; on desktop this may not be available via GetForCurrentView\n            try\n            {\n                _displayInformation = DisplayInformation.GetForCurrentView();\n            }\n            catch (Exception)\n            {\n                _displayInformation = null;\n            }\n\n            // Populate orientation variables with the current state\n            _displayOrientation = _displayInformation?.CurrentOrientation ?? DisplayOrientations.Landscape;\n            if (_orientationSensor != null)\n            {\n                _deviceOrientation = _orientationSensor.GetCurrentOrientation();\n            }\n\n            // Try to obtain system media transport controls\n            try\n            {\n                _systemMediaControls = SystemMediaTransportControls.GetForCurrentView();\n            }\n            catch (Exception)\n            {\n                _systemMediaControls = null;\n            }\n\n            RegisterEventHandlers();\n\n            var picturesLibrary = await StorageLibrary.GetLibraryAsync(KnownLibraryId.Pictures);\n            // Fall back to the local app storage if the Pictures Library is not available\n            _captureFolder = picturesLibrary.SaveFolder ?? ApplicationData.Current.LocalFolder;\n        }\n\n        /// <summary>\n        /// Unregisters event handlers for hardware buttons and orientation sensors, allows the StatusBar (on Phone) to show, and removes the page orientation lock\n        /// </summary>\n        /// <returns></returns>\n        private Task CleanupUiAsync()\n        {\n            UnregisterEventHandlers();\n\n            // Revert orientation preferences\n            DisplayInformation.AutoRotationPreferences = DisplayOrientations.None;\n\n            return Task.CompletedTask;\n        }\n\n        /// <summary>\n        /// This method will enable/disable the buttons depending on the current state of the app\n        /// </summary>\n        private void UpdateUi()\n        {\n            // The buttons should only be enabled if the preview started sucessfully\n            PhotoButton.IsEnabled = _isPreviewing;\n            CycleModeButton.IsEnabled = _isPreviewing;\n        }\n\n        /// <summary>\n        /// Registers event handlers for hardware buttons and orientation sensors, and performs an initial update of the UI rotation\n        /// </summary>\n        private void RegisterEventHandlers()\n        {\n\n            // If there is an orientation sensor present on the device, register for notifications\n            if (_orientationSensor != null)\n            {\n                _orientationSensor.OrientationChanged += OrientationSensor_OrientationChanged;\n\n                // Update orientation of buttons with the current orientation\n                UpdateControlOrientation();\n            }\n\n            // Have the Advanced Capture button call the method that cycles through the modes\n            CycleModeButton.Click += (sender, args) => CycleAdvancedCaptureMode();\n\n            if (_displayInformation != null)\n            {\n                _displayInformation.OrientationChanged += DisplayInformation_OrientationChanged;\n            }\n\n            if (_systemMediaControls != null)\n            {\n                _systemMediaControls.PropertyChanged += SystemMediaControls_PropertyChanged;\n            }\n        }\n\n        /// <summary>\n        /// Unregisters event handlers for hardware buttons and orientation sensors\n        /// </summary>\n        private void UnregisterEventHandlers()\n        {\n\n            if (_orientationSensor != null)\n            {\n                _orientationSensor.OrientationChanged -= OrientationSensor_OrientationChanged;\n            }\n\n            if (_displayInformation != null)\n            {\n                _displayInformation.OrientationChanged -= DisplayInformation_OrientationChanged;\n            }\n\n            if (_systemMediaControls != null)\n            {\n                _systemMediaControls.PropertyChanged -= SystemMediaControls_PropertyChanged;\n            }\n        }\n\n        /// <summary>\n        /// Attempts to find and return a device mounted on the panel specified, and on failure to find one it will return the first device listed\n        /// </summary>\n        /// <param name=\"desiredPanel\">The desired panel on which the returned device should be mounted, if available</param>\n        /// <returns></returns>\n        private static async Task<DeviceInformation?> FindCameraDeviceByPanelAsync(Windows.Devices.Enumeration.Panel desiredPanel)\n        {\n            // Get available devices for capturing pictures\n            var allVideoDevices = await DeviceInformation.FindAllAsync(DeviceClass.VideoCapture);\n\n            // Get the desired camera by panel\n            DeviceInformation? desiredDevice = allVideoDevices.FirstOrDefault(x => x.EnclosureLocation != null && x.EnclosureLocation.Panel == desiredPanel);\n\n            // If there is no device mounted on the desired panel, return the first device found\n            return desiredDevice ?? allVideoDevices.FirstOrDefault();\n        }\n\n        /// <summary>\n        /// Applies the given orientation to a photo stream and saves it as a StorageFile\n        /// </summary>\n        /// <param name=\"stream\">The photo stream</param>\n        /// <param name=\"file\">The StorageFile in which the photo stream will be saved</param>\n        /// <param name=\"photoOrientation\">The orientation metadata to apply to the photo</param>\n        /// <returns></returns>\n        private static async Task ReencodeAndSavePhotoAsync(IRandomAccessStream stream, StorageFile file, PhotoOrientation photoOrientation)\n        {\n            using (var inputStream = stream)\n            {\n                var decoder = await BitmapDecoder.CreateAsync(inputStream);\n\n                using (var outputStream = await file.OpenAsync(FileAccessMode.ReadWrite))\n                {\n                    var encoder = await BitmapEncoder.CreateForTranscodingAsync(outputStream, decoder);\n\n                    var properties = new BitmapPropertySet { { \"System.Photo.Orientation\", new BitmapTypedValue(photoOrientation, PropertyType.UInt16) } };\n\n                    await encoder.BitmapProperties.SetPropertiesAsync(properties);\n                    await encoder.FlushAsync();\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        #endregion Helper methods\n\n\n        #region Rotation helpers\n\n        /// <summary>\n        /// Calculates the current camera orientation from the device orientation by taking into account whether the camera is external or facing the user\n        /// </summary>\n        /// <returns>The camera orientation in space, with an inverted rotation in the case the camera is mounted on the device and is facing the user</returns>\n        private SimpleOrientation GetCameraOrientation()\n        {\n            if (_externalCamera)\n            {\n                // Cameras that are not attached to the device do not rotate along with it, so apply no rotation\n                return SimpleOrientation.NotRotated;\n            }\n\n            var result = _deviceOrientation;\n\n            // Account for the fact that, on portrait-first devices, the camera sensor is mounted at a 90 degree offset to the native orientation\n            if (_displayInformation?.NativeOrientation == DisplayOrientations.Portrait)\n            {\n                switch (result)\n                {\n                    case SimpleOrientation.Rotated90DegreesCounterclockwise:\n                        result = SimpleOrientation.NotRotated;\n                        break;\n                    case SimpleOrientation.Rotated180DegreesCounterclockwise:\n                        result = SimpleOrientation.Rotated90DegreesCounterclockwise;\n                        break;\n                    case SimpleOrientation.Rotated270DegreesCounterclockwise:\n                        result = SimpleOrientation.Rotated180DegreesCounterclockwise;\n                        break;\n                    case SimpleOrientation.NotRotated:\n                        result = SimpleOrientation.Rotated270DegreesCounterclockwise;\n                        break;\n                }\n            }\n\n            // If the preview is being mirrored for a front-facing camera, then the rotation should be inverted\n            if (_mirroringPreview)\n            {\n                // This only affects the 90 and 270 degree cases, because rotating 0 and 180 degrees is the same clockwise and counter-clockwise\n                switch (result)\n                {\n                    case SimpleOrientation.Rotated90DegreesCounterclockwise:\n                        return SimpleOrientation.Rotated270DegreesCounterclockwise;\n                    case SimpleOrientation.Rotated270DegreesCounterclockwise:\n                        return SimpleOrientation.Rotated90DegreesCounterclockwise;\n                }\n            }\n\n            return result;\n        }\n\n        /// <summary>\n        /// Converts the given orientation of the device in space to the corresponding rotation in degrees\n        /// </summary>\n        /// <param name=\"orientation\">The orientation of the device in space</param>\n        /// <returns>An orientation in degrees</returns>\n        private static int ConvertDeviceOrientationToDegrees(SimpleOrientation orientation)\n        {\n            switch (orientation)\n            {\n                case SimpleOrientation.Rotated90DegreesCounterclockwise:\n                    return 90;\n                case SimpleOrientation.Rotated180DegreesCounterclockwise:\n                    return 180;\n                case SimpleOrientation.Rotated270DegreesCounterclockwise:\n                    return 270;\n                case SimpleOrientation.NotRotated:\n                default:\n                    return 0;\n            }\n        }\n\n        /// <summary>\n        /// Converts the given orientation of the app on the screen to the corresponding rotation in degrees\n        /// </summary>\n        /// <param name=\"orientation\">The orientation of the app on the screen</param>\n        /// <returns>An orientation in degrees</returns>\n        private static int ConvertDisplayOrientationToDegrees(DisplayOrientations orientation)\n        {\n            switch (orientation)\n            {\n                case DisplayOrientations.Portrait:\n                    return 90;\n                case DisplayOrientations.LandscapeFlipped:\n                    return 180;\n                case DisplayOrientations.PortraitFlipped:\n                    return 270;\n                case DisplayOrientations.Landscape:\n                default:\n                    return 0;\n            }\n        }\n\n        /// <summary>\n        /// Converts the given orientation of the device in space to the metadata that can be added to captured photos\n        /// </summary>\n        /// <param name=\"orientation\">The orientation of the device in space</param>\n        /// <returns></returns>\n        private static PhotoOrientation ConvertOrientationToPhotoOrientation(SimpleOrientation orientation)\n        {\n            switch (orientation)\n            {\n                case SimpleOrientation.Rotated90DegreesCounterclockwise:\n                    return PhotoOrientation.Rotate90;\n                case SimpleOrientation.Rotated180DegreesCounterclockwise:\n                    return PhotoOrientation.Rotate180;\n                case SimpleOrientation.Rotated270DegreesCounterclockwise:\n                    return PhotoOrientation.Rotate270;\n                case SimpleOrientation.NotRotated:\n                default:\n                    return PhotoOrientation.Normal;\n            }\n        }\n\n        /// <summary>\n        /// Uses the current device orientation in space and page orientation on the screen to calculate the rotation\n        /// transformation to apply to the controls\n        /// </summary>\n        private void UpdateControlOrientation()\n        {\n            int device = ConvertDeviceOrientationToDegrees(_deviceOrientation);\n            int display = ConvertDisplayOrientationToDegrees(_displayOrientation);\n\n            if (_displayInformation?.NativeOrientation == DisplayOrientations.Portrait)\n            {\n                device -= 90;\n            }\n\n            // Combine both rotations and make sure that 0 <= result < 360\n            var angle = (360 + display + device) % 360;\n\n            // Rotate the buttons in the UI to match the rotation of the device\n            var transform = new RotateTransform { Angle = angle };\n\n            // The RenderTransform is safe to use (i.e. it won't cause layout issues) in this case, because these buttons have a 1:1 aspect ratio\n            PhotoButton.RenderTransform = transform;\n            CycleModeButton.RenderTransform = transform;\n\n            // Adjust which way the progress bar fills so it's always from the user's left->right or bottom->top\n            HdrImpactBar.FlowDirection = (angle == 180 || angle == 270) ? FlowDirection.RightToLeft : FlowDirection.LeftToRight;\n        }\n\n        #endregion Rotation helpers\n    }\n}\n```\n\n## CHANGE 4: Replace Package.appxmanifest\n\nReplace the entire content of 57.                 rootFrame = new Frame();
58.                 // Set the default language
59.                 rootFrame.Language = Windows.Globalization.ApplicationLanguages.Languages[0];
60. 
61.                 rootFrame.NavigationFailed += OnNavigationFailed;
62. 
63.                 // Place the frame in the current Window
64.                 // TODO[migrate-002]: see PATTERNS.md#windowing
65.                 Window.Current.Content = rootFrame;
66.             }
67. 
68.             if (rootFrame.Content == null)
69.             {
70.                 // When the navigation stack isn't restored navigate to the first page,
71.                 // configuring the new page by passing required information as a navigation
72.                 // parameter
73.                 rootFrame.Navigate(typeof(MainPage), e.Arguments);
74.             }
75.             // Ensure the current window is active
76.             // TODO[migrate-003]: see PATTERNS.md#windowing
77.             Window.Current.Activate();
78.         }
79. 
80.         /// <summary>
81.         /// Invoked when Navigation to a certain page fails
82.         /// </summary>
83.         /// <param name="sender">The Frame which failed navigation</param>
84.         /// <param name="e">Details about the navigation failure</param>
85.         void OnNavigationFailed(object sender, NavigationFailedEventArgs e)
86.         {
87.             throw new Exception("Failed to load Page " + e.SourcePageType.FullName);
88.         }
89.     }
90. }

This allows users to take pictures of documents (FaceUp)\n                // or the ceiling (FaceDown) in portrait or landscape, by first holding the device in the desired orientation, and then pointing the camera\n                // either up or down, at the desired subject.\n                //Note: This assumes that the camera is either facing the same way as the screen, or the opposite way.       });\n        }\n\n        /// <summary>\n        /// This event will be raised only on devices that support returning a reference photo, which is a normal exposure of the scene\n        ///
